# KOReader SDL reference and control matrix

This is a fresh comparison against genuine pinned KOReader UI running on this Mac. It does not recover the missing original Oasis screenshots or establish Kindle hardware acceptance. The Rust comparison is frozen at `ff03a14609bad09cdb390776ec93190d07ffbbea`; it predates the current scope trim. Its source hashes remain historical evidence. OPDS acquisition and remote progress sync from that baseline have been removed from the current source candidate and remain excluded from the completion denominator.

Current evidence for `T3-6.1` through `T3-6.3` covers Back in Reader and the file browser, exit policies, and independent jump and recent-view history. `T3-6.4` remains a keyboard-input gap. [Back navigation](back-navigation.md)

Current evidence for `T4-4.2.3` through `T4-4.2.8` includes the four stock starting weekdays, ranked book spans, hourly calendar bins, a minute-based timeline start and a separate calendar shift. The earlier matrix retains the captured baseline. [Calendar and timeline settings](reading-statistics.md#calendar-and-timeline-settings)

The follow-up for `T4-4.2.1` compares the stock page duration editor and the matched whole-second workflow in Reader. It verifies tap, hold, direct entry, defaults, Cancel, Apply and elapsed-time boundaries. [Page duration limits](reading-statistics.md#page-duration-limits)

The follow-up for `T4-4.6` captures the seven-day widget in genuine pinned KOReader and verifies matching weekly, daily and session totals in Reader. [Seven-day progress](reading-statistics.md#seven-day-reading-progress)

## Scope and reproducibility

The accepted run `20261122T090105Z` contains **128 unmodified full-screen PNGs at 1264 × 1680 framebuffer pixels, 300 DPI**: one reading page, 117 top-menu pages, six bottom panels, one selection popup and three dictionary states. A fresh English profile opens an authored shared EPUB and two authored StarDict dictionaries through ordinary `reader.lua`. Selection uses native source-word geometry; dictionary results come from the built native sdcv. The real Tools menu has two root pages, and both are captured. Top submenu enumeration follows enabled literal tables to depth two. Dynamic factories and leaf setting/network/power actions are inventoried without invocation. The file-browser root icon is an action that closes Reader, so it is visible and inventoried but not invoked.

The second run `20261122T091644Z` records actual Lua function provenance using `debug.getinfo`; it produces no replacement PNGs. All 128 metadata views match their original image by surface, category, submenu indices, page/panel and normalized label. There are **717 unique top rows**, deduplicated by category, submenu indices, row index and text. The rendered menu tables expose no stable stock item IDs, so IDs below are derived audit identities. Metadata contains row-specific source functions for 638 rows; 78 literal navigation rows without a row-specific function use the genuine generic `TouchMenu:onMenuSelect` handler; one empty-directory placeholder has no action callback. All recorded handler roles remain in the [machine-readable matrix](koreader-sdl-reference-controls.json), including callback, hold, enabled, checked, text and dynamic submenu functions.

All six bottom panels contribute **24 source option records**. Twenty-two are actually visible: CJK scaling is hidden behind Word Expansion's More Options, and image inversion is conditional on night mode. Embedded Fonts is visible but disabled for the authored fixture, which has no embedded fonts. A source-only record is not a control seen in that image. The dictionary frames show the actual title/source header, query edit icon, result count, scroll area, vocabulary button, source navigation, Highlight, Wikipedia, Search and Close; these must not be mistaken for a text-only definition panel.

Local evidence lives in the ignored `build/parity-review/koreader-macos-reference/` tree. Open `runs/20261122T090105Z/index.html` for the complete gallery; frame numbers below identify the exact PNG in the JSON manifest. The repository retains hashes, counts, source links and original comparison prose, rather than publishing 128 stock screenshots or owner files. Public metadata replaces only QA/profile absolute paths. Decorative private-use glyphs are retained in JSON and omitted from readable labels without inventing icon meanings. The original images and raw event logs remain unchanged locally.

| Input or result | Exact identity |
| --- | --- |
| KOReader | `9192014d8bd82a91dc1012473be0f238dedfdb54` (`v2026.07.1`) |
| koreader-base | `6e4bc81af4e04f78d81677a323a780a71b29702a` |
| CREngine | `b32a88ffbbf505cf2ea5208c1a49002d70d3609f` |
| Fonts source | `046976988aa33639d60d6ffd25c7a0ff50b72ac0` |
| Meson 1.9.1 | `751b09390996163348612f0b106114256305ee40` |
| Approved source/tool manifest SHA-256 | `3c47d75cc6dcaa7e6b8621aaeee390657e6e2d5353e3123ae0d22fa16fe386c9` |
| Authored EPUB SHA-256 | `80f163e4ee608913ecbf482a44271f52907cdb1b4027114464ebd9083f728bf3` |
| Runtime freeze SHA-256 | `53719081e7193d5b184ab0cea142ad5c5095d02cdf4b23becabe6f1f5971c126` |

The isolated build used pinned GNU Make 4.4.1, coreutils 9.12 and findutils 4.11.0, the CLT compiler17 and its selected SDK headers. Sources, tools, downloads, licences and runtime remain under the QA tree; no global package installation was performed. Upstream KOReader/base AGPL and dependency licences remain separate from Reader's package licence. The authored harness and fixtures carry their own project licence. No Lua implementation or rule dataset was copied into Reader. The runtime freeze records 1,863 logical files, 1,819 unique targets, 45 symlinks and 262,073,049 bytes; external or cyclic runtime links were absent. After all capture processes exited and `lsof` was clear, only the approved 671,080,448 bytes of completed build scratch were removed, with all frozen targets and links verified before and after. Build retry fixes, checksums, source pins and cleanup receipts stay local in `provenance/`.

The native SDL window is 632 × 840 logical points on this Retina Mac; the accepted framebuffer is measured, never resized. An earlier rejected 2528 × 2102 diagnostic run remains separately labelled. SDL Emulator capabilities, macOS platform entries, existing host fonts, rasterizers and the authored profile differ from Oasis. Host fonts were read by stock KOReader but were not copied into Reader. These frames establish UI structure and observed enabled/visible states. They do **not** prove leaf callbacks, e-ink waveforms, Kindle input transforms, sleep/USB behavior or full engine parity. [Reading acceptance plan](reading-parity-plan.md)

## How to read the matrix

Each row pairs one stock control with its genuine frame and pinned handler. **workflow** means the corresponding native reading workflow exists with linked source/tests; it does not claim pixel equality or every stock fine option. **partial** identifies a concrete narrower implementation or different contract. **gap** names an absent or unfulfilled exact contract. **open-audit** means the exact preference has not been matched, rather than asserting all related functionality is absent. **group** is literal submenu navigation, whose descendants are listed separately. **system-owned** assigns system policy to Launcher without claiming every advanced stock system option. **excluded** marks non-reading utilities and network integrations outside the current local-reading goal, retained here for source context only. Excluded rows are closed scope, not implementation tasks, blockers or completion-denominator entries. Counts are inventory counts, not a parity percentage.

The evidence anchors describe the native area and point to source, focused tests, authored screenshots or original-file receipts already present at the frozen baseline. A family link is related evidence; it does not upgrade an **open-audit** row to acceptance. Stock callback metadata is source provenance, not an execution receipt. Dynamic submenu contents, nested menus beyond depth two, disabled branches, fixed-page-only provider panels and alternate device/night/language profiles require separate runs. This is a comprehensive inventory of this captured scope, not the complete KOReader product catalog.

## Concrete remaining reading gaps

1. Compare the four kerning modes, percent line-spacing presets/fine bounds, and logical font-size scale/range/half steps on Kindle. Word spacing implements the stock scaling/reduction pair, presets, custom bounds and line fitting ([details](word-spacing.md)); compare the resulting layouts on Kindle. Rust kerning modes expose the stock choices, but Fast uses reduced Rust shaping rather than FreeType's dedicated path ([details](kerning-modes.md)).
2. Complete both landscape orientations and input transforms; implement the box, float and pagination mechanisms measured in the [Legacy, Flat, Book and Web audit](render-modes.md).
3. Separate the independent **top engine header** from footer/timer output. Implement the stock footer item catalog, per-item composition and geometry where required; current cycling/combined metrics cover only a subset.
4. Extend the independent [Embedded fonts control](embedded-font-families.md#embedded-fonts-control) to show registered and used families and verify availability beyond the bounded inventory. Keep the custom render-DPI picker (0–300), exact hinting/default behavior, full typography-language catalog, UI localization/mirroring and in-page footnote styling explicit.
5. Finish reading-specific option catalogs: contents action/row settings, selection HTML diagnostic views, dictionary query-edit/highlight controls and the remaining popup preferences. Bookmark ordering/row text, configurable long-press actions, selection Copy/Paste and popup placement now have native controls. Existing annotation workflows must remain intact.
6. The frozen Rust baseline included daily reading history, local exports and WebDAV sync. Keep stock time-range/reset/cloud features, native KOReader sync-server interoperability, service exporters, Wikipedia EPUB export and arbitrary export-path settings distinct. Remote acquisition and sync have since been removed from the current source and remain excluded.

Stock font sizes are **logical**, not physical pixels: pinned [readerfont.lua:203](https://github.com/koreader/koreader/blob/9192014d8bd82a91dc1012473be0f238dedfdb54/frontend/apps/reader/modules/readerfont.lua#L203) clamps 12–255 then calls `Screen:scaleBySize`; [base/ffi/framebuffer.lua:414](https://github.com/koreader/koreader-base/blob/6e4bc81af4e04f78d81677a323a780a71b29702a/ffi/framebuffer.lua#L414) combines the minimum screen dimension with an explicit DPI override when present. With no override, physical DPI does not independently enter that formula. Consequently a stock label 22 cannot be compared with native 22 pixels or assumed to equal `22*dpi/160`. Margin numeric labels likewise have different units. Image similarity alone cannot establish these contracts.


## Additional native footnote oracle

After the image matrix checkpoint, `footnote-oracle-20261122T095046Z` ran four newly authored controls through the same pinned runtime in a fresh profile, with popup footnotes enabled and Prefer more footnotes disabled. This run wrote no PNGs. All original 128 images and their hashes remain unchanged. Actual page-link geometry was resolved through `ReaderLink:getLinkFromGes`, then the ordinary `ReaderLink:onTap` path ran. The native classifier received flags `0xdf7e` and a 10,000-character limit; all four returned true because the source had `epub:type=noteref`, with “trusted container, no need” for extension. The actual popup extraction call was `getHTMLFromXPointer(target, 0x1001, true)` and a real `FootnoteWidget` was created without changing the source position. This is controller/extraction evidence, not a claim of text visibility or Kindle acceptance.

| Target and hiding rule | Actual exported container/style |
| --- | --- |
| A: `id` on inner P; ancestor aside has inline `display:none` | Only P is exported. The ancestor aside and its inline style are absent. |
| B: `id` on the inline-hidden aside | The aside and inline `style="display:none"` survive, surrounding its P. |
| C: `id` on inner P; ancestor aside uses linked `.note { display:none }` | Only P is exported. |
| D: `id` on the linked-hidden aside | The aside and class survive; the linked stylesheet is omitted. |

The native API does not unconditionally widen a target to a semantic aside. It chooses a rendered final ancestor only when that ancestor exists. Reader's unconditional widening at the frozen comparison baseline therefore remains a concrete extraction gap. The export flags can insert discretionary soft hyphens; they are not changes to the source fixture. `FootnoteWidget` also rewrites exported IDs internally, which is separate from the direct API result. Exact normalized targets, HTML, empty CSS lists, native decisions and widget/source-position observations are retained in the JSON matrix. [Native extraction](https://github.com/koreader/koreader-base/blob/6e4bc81af4e04f78d81677a323a780a71b29702a/cre.cpp#L2785) · [Stock popup policy and extraction](https://github.com/koreader/koreader/blob/9192014d8bd82a91dc1012473be0f238dedfdb54/frontend/apps/reader/modules/readerlink.lua#L1408)

The authored EPUB SHA-256 is `90c34122680e29a056c92a591fae62db779d352c801e50a1b81bd5e60c2c52e7`; the receipt SHA-256 is `d5c993464505fe1022cbbb6080aaa4f42526ed3917d45a4ebbcc610ade926b5a`. The complete frozen runtime/file/link inventory and authored fixture were verified unchanged before and after. All processes exited; no owner documents, build cache or device was used.

The extended run `footnote-oracle-20261122T095415Z` repeats A–D and adds four ordinary inline/container cases, again using actual geometry taps and native popup construction. All eight retain the source position. Its fixture SHA-256 is `84fff6bd39157018dd1c3a2495996bf6ee95a3cd6edcc1b955612f9874a9b981`; receipt SHA-256 is `8896dec6f33731e5bc87088ac73ed965bcb26e97b29e11d6a2c11f91044092a1`. The runtime and fixture remain unchanged, with no PNGs or device acceptance.

| Additional target context | Actual exported root |
| --- | --- |
| E: visible span inside a visible paragraph | Whole P, including text before and after the target. |
| F: span inside P inside a `display:none` block div | Original span only. Choosing the nearest P would add context that stock does not export. |
| G: em inside a hidden inline span inside an otherwise visible P | Whole visible P, retaining the hidden span and its inline style. A hidden ancestor does not stop the upward search. |
| H: visible span inside a div containing only inline flow | Whole div. |

The exact native algorithm is an inclusive target-to-root search for the first node with render method `erm_final`; if none exists, export the original target. It does not select a semantic note ancestor, stop at `erm_invisible`, or equate every block tag with a final render node. It then resets the node offset to zero for serialization. A full match therefore requires actual rendered final-block ownership and the separate classifier/extended-range policy. A correction that only removes unconditional semantic-aside widening fixes A/C while leaving other CSS/render-final cases explicitly scoped. [Inclusive final-node search](https://github.com/koreader/crengine/blob/b32a88ffbbf505cf2ea5208c1a49002d70d3609f/crengine/src/lvtinydom.cpp#L9983)

## Native evidence index

### navigation

**Reading navigation.** Contents, bookmarks, go-to, map, page previews, skim, automatic turns and return history have native entry points. Individual stock presentation preferences remain separate.

[src/app/top_config.rs](../src/app/top_config.rs) · [src/app/reading_bars.rs](../src/app/reading_bars.rs) · [src/app.rs](../src/app.rs) · [reading-control-evidence.md](reading-control-evidence.md)

### contents

**Contents.** Publisher outlines have branch controls and reveal the current path. Publisher and custom headings support title search with ancestor context and preserve their original destinations. The generated outline remains available. The Contents list supports persistent row count and logical entry font preferences. Page labels, dot leaders, and known chapter lengths appear in Contents. Depth selection applies to chapter jumps and to progress markers where page coordinates are available.

[Searchable contents and simulator captures](searchable-contents.md) · [Page labels and chapter length evidence](contents-row-metadata.md) · [src/app/custom_contents.rs](../src/app/custom_contents.rs) · [reading-control-evidence.md](reading-control-evidence.md)

### bookmarks

**Bookmarks and notes.** Book-local marks/search and cross-book History, selected-book, collection, archived and metadata filtering are implemented. The in-book Saved marks view combines EPUB/PDF page bookmarks and highlights, sorts by reading location or saved date, and retains original action targets. Highlight rows support note-first, highlighted-text, and text-with-note display modes. The in-book list has saved row and font preferences. Additional layout options and Kindle acceptance remain open. [Bookmark and annotation lists](bookmark-page-order.md)

### network-extra

**Optional reading services (excluded).** The frozen Rust baseline contained OPDS acquisition and WebDAV progress-sync providers. Both were removed from the current source candidate; the upstream KOReader controls remain here as reference evidence only, not gaps to implement.

[opds-acquisition.md](opds-acquisition.md) · [progress-sync.md](progress-sync.md) · [requirements.md](requirements.md)

### excluded

**Outside core reading scope.** Terminal, RSS downloader, HTTP inspector/server administration and unrelated utilities are excluded by the reading-parity requirements; they remain inventoried rather than counted as implemented.

[reading-parity-plan.md](reading-parity-plan.md) · [requirements.md](requirements.md)

### unmapped

**Open exact-control audit.** No exact native mapping was established for this captured preference. Related functionality must not be treated as matching this control without a focused source and behavior check.

[reading-parity-plan.md](reading-parity-plan.md)
